- **Limits.** `locks` (200, at most 10,000), `lock_capacity` (64, at most 1,000), `lock_waiters` (100) and `lock_waits_per_principal` (20), each at most 1,000. A principal's holds have no cap of their own: `locks` times `lock_capacity` bounds them.
- **Telemetry and logs.** `aries.locks` counts the lock events and renewals by `event`, and `aries.lock.wait.duration` how long a granted place waited, on the store's clock; its buckets are every histogram's, so waits past 10 seconds fall in the last. `aries.limit.refusals` gains the three caps that refuse `busy`. **A lock's name is a person's text** (§16.4): it is on no metric and no span, a request's span carrying only the op as for every request, and on the log lines `lock.expired` and `lock.released` it is the field `lock`, of kind local, which stays in the journal and is never exported over OTLP.
- **The CLI.** `aries lock acquire|renew|release|leave|list|run`. `acquire` prints the epoch alone, and with `--wait` waits until granted, renewing its place every 30 seconds and giving it up on an interrupt. `lock run NAME [--wait] -- CMD` holds the lock around CMD with `ARIES_LOCK_EPOCH` set, renews at a third of the lease (and at a sixth after a renewal that did not arrive), releases when CMD exits and exits with its code. **An interrupt** is passed to CMD, which is killed after 10 seconds, and the hold released once it stopped; aries exits 130 if CMD died of it. **A hold the server says ended** (its lease ran out while the server could not be reached, or access ended) stops CMD the same way, and aries exits 1: §20.5 did not say, and fail closed was chosen over letting CMD run on unguarded. **aries killed outright** releases nothing: the lease ends the hold and the reaper grants it on. Each run takes a session of its own (the default session and `.lock-` with a random suffix), so two runs in one terminal are two holders, not one. A waiting run watches its inbox, so the `lock.granted` push wakes it at once, and it marks that item read. `cli.Env` gains `Environ`, CMD's environment, and `After`, the clock the lock commands wait on, which the end-to-end tests run as fast as a fast store clock.
- **Protocol.** None raised: the ops `lock.acquire`, `lock.renew`, `lock.release`, `lock.leave` and `locks` joined unreleased protocol 8, as G0's and G1's did (decision 78). **Offline:** no lock op is queued, and `locks` is refused offline as `ready` is; `aries lock run` runs nothing when the server cannot be reached. **Agents:** no tool, and no schema changed; `docs/agents.md` says agents do not take locks.
- **The gate.** The soak (`soak_locks_test.go`, part of every run, short and long) has nine sessions contend for `train/main` (capacity 1) and `pool` (capacity 3) in the first project, with a lease of a minute on the store's clock, through the run's dropped answers, dropped connections, restarts, clock steps and access changes; sessions die holding or waiting, and stall past their leases. The replay checks each transaction of the log: no grant at capacity, a grant only to the first live waiter, an acquire at once only to an empty queue, every epoch the project's last plus one, nothing ended before its lease, and no slot left free while someone waits; each snapshot holds the lock tables to the replay; no epoch is told to two sessions; and every lapsed hold is ended within two reaper ticks and 7 seconds of real time after its lease (3 seconds until 15 Oct 2026, when CI's shared runner under the race detector ended three holds 3.4 seconds late), less any jump of the clock settled meanwhile (a lease that ran out before a jump had all its time, and is ended on the time after it), unless a restart fell between. **Runs, 15 Oct 2026, on a laptop (arm64):** short runs (12 seconds; one under the race detector) passed; a first run of four minutes failed on 20 holds that lapsed before a jump forward and were measured with the jump, which the check now leaves out, and on one ended about 2 seconds of real time late, past the 1.7 seconds the check allowed then; the same seed of four minutes then passed (742 holds, 470 acquires and 427 grants in the log, 477 expired, peak holders 1 and 3, lapsed holds ended p50 66 ms, p99 470 ms, max 751 ms after their lease in real time), and so did five minutes under the race detector (929 holds; 610 acquires, 512 grants, 491 releases of which 42 for lost access, 631 expired, 107 leaves; p50 68 ms, p99 498 ms, max 1.09 s; 1088 dropped answers, 138 dropped connections, 2 restarts). The report line `locks:` of any run gives the same counts.
- **Not built in G2:** locks shared by projects (§20.5, deferred); an admin's release of another's hold; paging of `locks`. Not measured: how long a lock takes to pass on under heavy contention at the real 30-second tick (§20.14), and the soak's at its 100 ms tick only. Not run: `aries lock run` on Windows, where an interrupt cannot be sent and CMD is killed at once.

**As built (gates' leftovers before v1.0.0, 16 Oct 2026).** What G0 and G1 left open, settled on the way to the release:
- **Runs out of order.** G1 took the last report as the truth, so a slow run of an older commit that failed after a newer one passed reopened the gate and held the work back again. A report now carries its CI run's number, `run` on `gate.pass` and `gate.fail` (`GateArgs.Run`, kept in the report), which `aries` reads from `GITHUB_RUN_NUMBER` or `CI_PIPELINE_IID`, or `--run N`: each rises with every run of the workflow or pipeline and is kept when a run is retried. The rule: a report of a lower run than the gate's last report changes nothing, in either direction, with one exception that fails closed: a failure of the commit that last passed (`sha`, either abbreviated) reopens the gate whatever its run. Reports that do not both carry a run are taken in the order they arrive, as in G1, as are reruns of one run; a rearm forgets the last report's run, so the order starts again, as it must for a workflow whose numbering does. Chosen over the run's start time, which neither GitHub Actions nor GitLab CI gives a job and which would rest on runners' clocks, and over comparing commits, which the server cannot order. GitHub numbers each workflow's runs apart, so one check is reported from one workflow (docs/ci.md says so). A report that loses is answered with the gate as it stands, which the CLI explains on stderr and exits 0. **Protocol:** `run` is a new argument, a protocol change under rule 10; no release has carried protocol 8, so on decisions 78 and 88 it joins 8 with no bump, and a server built before it refuses it as an unknown field.
- **A gate that names no approvers tells its admins.** G0's gate told nobody, since the store asked `IsAdmin` of one principal at a time. `Options.ListAdmins` now lists them, which a server of many projects answers from the control store's index in memory (`Control.ProjectAdmins`: the project's admins by name, then the server admins, each active), and a single project from `admins:`. `gate.pending` goes to them, less the gate's creator, who may not pass it (decision 68), and at most `approvers_per_gate` of them, the project's own first, so a server of many admins tells a bounded few: the cap a gate's own list has, rather than a new setting. **And when it holds more:** a `dep add` that puts an issue directly behind an open human gate tells whom it waits for, named or admins, with the issue named (`gate.pending` again); the same edge added twice tells nobody twice. Work that a gate holds through a parent link or a blocker's edge tells nobody, as it did not before: those are many and are the gate's plan, which its approvers saw when it was added. The notices count against the sender's `notices_per_minute`, as every `gate.pending` does.
- **Metrics and log events** (§20.9, owed by G0 and G1). `aries.gates` counts each change of a gate by `kind` (`human`, `ci`) and `event` (`added`, `passed`, `failed`, `waived`, `rearmed`), as §20.9 has it; and each is a log line at info, `gate.added`, `gate.passed`, `gate.failed`, `gate.waived` and `gate.rearmed`, with the fields `gate` (the id, of kind issue), `kind` and `by`, and for a ci gate `issue` (its subject), `check` and `run`. A check's name is text a person chose, so `check` is of kind local and stays in the journal, as a lock's name does; nothing else on the line is text. Both come from the store through `Observer.GateChanged`, once the write commits, with the gate as it left it and the request's context, so a report or a decision that changed nothing (a pass of a passed gate, a report of an older run, an idempotent add's replay) is neither counted nor logged. The observer now also logs, so ariesd gives each project store one whenever it has a log, telemetry or not (`StoreObserver(tel, log, project)`). §20.9's log event `gate.failed` is one of the five. `deploy/observability`'s dashboard has a panel of them; there is no alert, since how many gates a team passes or fails is its own business, not the server's health. The names are recorded in `TestNamesKept`'s golden files, as decision 88's promise allows additions.

## 21. A project's workflow: the flow Aries enforces

Proposed 16 Oct 2026 (aries-3yxddgjk), from §5, §12, §18, §20 and the flow this repository is built by. **Decided 16 Oct 2026 (decision 95): all fourteen recommendations of §21.12 accepted. Slices W0, W1, W2, W3 and W4 are built (§21.15); the rest is not yet.** Asked by the maintainer: Aries cannot mandate a flow of merge, review and testing for the agents working in a project, and decision 93 brings this design into the minor releases before v1.0.0. It reads beside §20 (gates), the organization memory design still to come (aries-iakie56e), the enterprise review's E2, E6 and E13, and Bearings §3.18 (policy as code), which it is meant to be the base of rather than a rival to.

In short: a project gets one setting, its **workflow**, a document a project admin applies with `aries admin workflow set FILE`, never over MCP. For each issue type it names **default acceptance items**, which the server adds to every issue it creates, so that removing one is a waiver on the record; **required checks**, such as the ci gate `landed` of §20.4, without which an issue cannot be finished, so that closed means merged; an optional **review** before close and **approval** before start, each a human gate; and a **close-out checklist** that `handoff` and `finish` show and the harness carries out. The server applies all of it at create, from the setting in force then, and records which version it applied, so the event log still explains every issue. Defaults for a whole server cascade to its projects as a floor that a project may add to and never loosen, which is the rule §3.18's `policy.apply` needs. A server that CI cannot reach, as the dogfood server on a laptop cannot, has `landed` reported by `aries land`, run by a named person after pulling the default branch. No MCP tool, no tool argument and no MCP schema token are added; the protocol gains two ops, one bump.

### 21.1 What exists today, and where it fails

This repository runs on three conventions, as the v0.5.0 issues show:

- **The pinned project memory `workflow`** (rev 3, 16 Oct 2026), ten steps, which `prime` shows every session: start and branch, test first, commit, a pull request from the template, `handoff` and never `finish`, never merge, merge and close together, clean up, a CI failure is yours, read-only tasks post and finish.
- **Four acceptance items typed onto every issue that changes the repository:** "PR opened from the template, saying what ran and what was left to CI"; "Tests run as AGENTS.md's Test tiers require for the change"; "CI green on the PR"; "Reviewed and merged by the maintainer". `finish` refuses while any is open (§7, As built for stage 3, acceptance), which is what keeps step 5 honest.
- **Gates** (§20.15): human gates and ci gates, with `landed` telling closed from landed, which nothing yet requires.

| Step of the memory | Held today by | Where it fails | Held, with this design, by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Start; branch; own worktree | `start`'s claim; `start` names a branch | The branch and the worktree are advice | Unchanged: Aries sees no worktree. The claim stays the guard |
| 2. Test first; the test tiers | The item "Tests run…" | Present only if the person who filed the issue typed it | A default item, on every issue of the types the setting names |
| 3. Commit with trailer | Nothing | The `Aries:` trailer is what `landed` reads | Unchanged; `aries land` and `--from-commit` report what they cannot find |
| 4. A PR from the template | The item "PR opened…" | As step 2 | A default item |
| 5. `handoff`, never `finish` | The item "Reviewed and merged…" left open | As step 2; and an agent may tick it | A required check: `finish` is refused until `landed` passes (§21.5) |
| 6. Never merge | The host's branch protection | Not Aries's to enforce | Unchanged |
| 7. Merge and close together | The coordinating session, by hand | A merged PR whose issue nobody finishes; a finished issue whose PR never merged | The `landed` pass closes the issue when nothing else is open (§21.5) |
| 8. Clean up | The memory | Read once, forgotten at the end | The close-out checklist, shown by `handoff` and `finish` (§21.7) |
| 9. A CI failure is yours | `gate.failed` in the holder's inbox, if a ci gate exists | No gate unless someone added one | Required checks make the gate (§21.5) |
| 10. Read-only tasks post and finish | The memory | Its issues carry the four items, which do not apply | A type whose workflow names no items or checks (Question 13) |

So the failures are of two kinds. What is typed by hand depends on whoever files the issue remembering, and an agent filing discovered work through `finish` never does. And nothing ties an issue's closing to what happened on the host: `close` is the closer's assertion (§20.1), and `landed` is recorded beside it, not required by it.

### 21.2 Principles kept, and what they rule out

- **The event log is the truth (principles 2, 3).** What the workflow added to an issue is written into the issue at create, in the create's own write, with the version of the setting in its event. A later change of the setting changes no issue already made; nothing is derived at read time from a setting the issue's log does not name.
- **Rights are checked by the server, and MCP stays small (rule 12, decision 12).** Setting the workflow is an admin's act over `aries admin`; no tool sets, reads or bypasses it. Agents meet it as acceptance items, refusals with a fix, and lines in results they already receive.
- **Enforced on the server, whatever the client.** Defaults and required checks apply to a `create` and a `finish` from a client of any protocol version, including one that never heard of a workflow: the server applies them in the write, and asks the client for nothing.
- **Fail closed and loud (principle 6).** An issue that requires a check the server has no report of is not finished; the refusal says which check, who may report it and the next step (rule 11).
- **The harness does the chores (principle 5).** A close-out step such as removing a worktree is the harness's; the server holds the text and shows it at the right moment, and does not pretend to know it was done.
- **No runner, no outbound call (decision 67).** Required checks are the ci gates of §20.4, reported by a client. ariesd still polls nothing and receives no webhook; §21.8's laptop answer is a client too.
- **What this rules out:** a rule language on the server; a workflow an agent can change or turn off; defaults recomputed on open issues when the setting changes; the server closing an issue on anything but a report a named principal made.

### 21.3 The setting

**Where it lives.**

| Option | For | Against |
|---|---|---|
| **A. A row of the project's `settings` table (recommended)** | The table exists in project migration 0001 (`name`, `value` JSON, `source`, `updated_at`, `rev`) and nothing uses it yet; the project's writer writes it with an event in the project's log, beside the issues it governs, so `create` reads it in its own transaction; a single-project server and a server of many projects store it alike | The organization's default (§21.9) is elsewhere, in the control store |
| B. A row of the control store, per project | One place for every project's setting, beside memberships | Every `create` reads across stores, or through the in-memory index, which then holds every project's workflow; the issue's log no longer holds the version it was made under |
| C. The pinned memory, parsed | Nothing new to learn | Memories are agents' to write (`remember`); a setting that an agent's `remember` can change enforces nothing |

**The document,** applied as a whole, in YAML or JSON. `aries` parses it, with the YAML parser it already links for `.aries.yaml`, and sends JSON, so ariesd gains no parser. What this repository would apply, with `alice` for its maintainer:

```yaml
# .aries/workflow.yaml: applied with `aries admin workflow set`
version: 1
ci: [ci-example]              # principals that may report a required check
types:
  default:                  # every type not named below
    acceptance:
      - PR opened from the template, saying what ran and what was left to CI
      - Tests run as AGENTS.md's Test tiers require for the change
      - text: CI green on the PR
        check: build        # ticked by the check's pass (§21.5)
      - text: Reviewed and merged by the maintainer
        check: landed
    checks: [landed]
    closeout:
      - Stop and remove any compose stack you started, never a shared one
      - Delete files you left outside the repository
      - Leave the session to be archived when its PR merges
  epic:
    acceptance: []
    checks: []
    approve: {approvers: [alice]}   # a human gate on the epic at create (§21.6)
    closeout:
      - git fetch --prune and git worktree prune; delete merged branches
      - Put the main checkout back on main; archive finished sessions
  chore:                    # read-only runs: post the result, then finish
    acceptance: [The result is posted where the issue says]
    checks: []
```

- **Keys.** `types` maps an issue type (`bug`, `feature`, `task`, `epic`, `chore`) or `default` to a **profile**: `acceptance` (items, each a line or a `{text, check}`), `checks` (required check names, `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}` as G1's), `review` and `approve` (§21.6), `closeout` (lines). A named type replaces `default` whole, so what a type gets is read in one place. `gate` cannot be named: a gate's lifecycle is §20's. `ci` names the principals that may report a required check.
- **Checked at set:** the types and keys are known, so a typo is refused rather than ignored; each line passes `internal/safetext` and the secrets lint (`internal/secretscan`), as a memory does, since it will be copied into issues; every named principal is a member of the project; a bound check is among its profile's `checks`; the caps of §21.10.
- **A version.** The row's `rev` is the workflow's version; `aries admin workflow set FILE --rev N` refuses a stale apply (rule 6). Each set records `workflow.set` with the document before and after.
- **The file belongs in the repository,** reviewed in a pull request like the code it governs, as formulas are (decision 71); the server never reads the repository. `aries admin workflow diff FILE` prints what an apply would change, and `aries workflow check FILE` exits 1 when the server's differs, for a CI job that can reach the server.

### 21.4 Default acceptance items, at create

**How they are added.**

| Option | For | Against |
|---|---|---|
| **A. Appended to the issue's acceptance text in the create's write (recommended)** | Items are already derived from the text (§7, As built for stage 3); `start`, `show`, `accept`, `finish`'s `ticked` and `waived`, the offline cache, conflicts and bd export need no change; S-3's rule already refuses an `update` that drops an open item, so an item leaves an issue only ticked or waived, on the record | The text grows a list the creator did not type; an item reworded in the text is a new item, the old one still to be waived, as today |
| B. Derived at read from the setting in force | Nothing written | A change of the setting moves open issues' criteria mid-work, and the issue's log no longer explains them |
| C. Rows of a table of their own | Defaults apart from the creator's | A second kind of item in every place items are read; nothing gained over A |

**Rules (A).**
- **Which writes.** `create`, the discovered issues of `finish`, and `graph.create` when G3 builds it. Not `ariesd import-bd`, whose issues are history; not templates, which get theirs when poured; not gates.
- **Order and duplicates.** The profile's items follow the creator's, and an item whose text the creator already gave (the same hash as `acceptance_state` keys) is not added twice.
- **Recorded.** The `issue.create` event's after state gains `workflow: {rev, type}` (and the organization's rev, §21.9), and the issue's `metadata` keeps the profile's required checks and gates, so a later change of the setting changes nothing on it.
- **A change of type** (`update` of `type`) adds, in the same write, the new profile's items and checks that the issue lacks, recorded the same way; what the old type added stays until ticked or waived. Question 3.
- **Waiving is the only way out,** with a reason (`accept` or `finish`'s `waived`), as for any item; `close --force` stays an admin's override with its `acceptance_overridden` record.
- **No new argument.** `create` takes nothing new; a client of protocol 8 gets the defaults. `create`'s result gains `items`, how many the workflow added, a result field (no bump), which the CLI prints on stderr beside `similar`.

### 21.5 Close means landed: required checks

A profile's `checks` are names of §20.4's ci gates. **An issue whose profile requires a check cannot be finished until a ci gate of that check, with the issue as subject, has passed.**

**The gate is made by the first report, not at create.** Making a ci gate per required check at every `create` would add one or two gate issues to every issue made. Instead, a report (`gate.pass` or `gate.fail` by subject and check) for an issue whose recorded profile requires that check, and that has no gate for it yet, makes the gate in the report's write, as `gate.add` would, with the workflow's `ci` principals as its `by`. A report for a check the profile does not require still needs a gate someone added, as in G1. So `aries gate pass --check landed --from-commit` works on every issue the workflow governs without anyone adding a gate.

**What `finish` does meanwhile.**

| Option | For | Against |
|---|---|---|
| **1. `finish` and `close` are refused while a required check has not passed; the passing report closes the issue (recommended)** | Closed means landed, for dependents, `ready`, digest and cost per outcome (enterprise review E8). It is already the memory's step 5 (handoff, never finish) and removes step 7's manual finish | An issue waits with no holder between handoff and merge; a team whose CI cannot report must have a way to (§21.8) |
| 2. `finish` closes as today, shown "closed, not landed"; dependents stay blocked until it lands | The agent's flow is unchanged | A closed issue that blocks is a new rule in `blockedCTE`, `blocked`, the board and the soak's replay; a closed issue whose PR never merges looks done in every list |
| 3. Display only, as G1 is | Nothing to build | What exists today; the maintainer asked for more |

**Option 1 in detail.**
- **The refusal.** `finish` and `close` of such an issue are refused with code `acceptance`, the code an open item already gets, naming the checks and their last reports, with the fix: `handoff` with the pull request's link and state `done`; the issue closes when the check passes. `aries mcp` words it as the next call. `close --force` by an admin and `gate.waive` (an admin, from `cli`, with a reason) remain the overrides, each recorded.
- **A duplicate, a won't-do or a superseded issue** will never be named by a commit, so it never lands. It closes by the same override, an admin's `close --force` with a reason (`duplicate of ID`); the alternative, a gate that passes when the issue it duplicates lands, was not taken: it would let any agent that adds a `duplicates` link close past the gate, and an admin's act is the one that is already on the record. The close event carries `checks_overridden` and, when the issue has `duplicates` or `supersedes` links, `folded_into` with the issues they name (As built, 21 Oct 2026: aries-dvm3innv; no protocol change, so no fixed set of reasons).
- **Waiting to land.** After the agent's `handoff` the issue stays `in_progress`, holding nothing once the claim is released or lapses (§7, As built, authorization and holds), with a last handoff of state `done`. `digest` and `prime` list such issues as **awaiting landing**, with their checks' last reports; `ready` leaves them out, as it does everything in progress.
- **Items bound to a check** (`{text, check}`) are ticked by that check's pass, in the pass's write, with `acceptance.tick` by the reporting principal; a failure after a pass unticks them (`acceptance.untick`), as a failure reopens the gate. An agent may still tick such an item itself; Question 6 asks whether only the check may.
- **The closing pass.** When a pass leaves the subject with every required check passed and no item open, the same write closes it: `issue.close` by the reporting principal with `reason: landed`, ending any claim, and `gate.passed` to the assignee and the last handoff's author. When an item is still open, nothing closes, and the inbox item says what is left; whoever finishes it then is not refused.
- **A revert.** A later failure of `landed` reopens the gate (G1) and, under option 1, reopens the issue (`issue.reopen` by the reporter, `reason: check failed`), telling its assignee, since closed meant landed. Question 5.

### 21.6 Review and approval

**Review before close.** A profile's `review: {approvers: [...]}` requires a person's pass before the issue can close, as a required check does: a human gate whose subject is the issue, made by the server in the create's write and holding nothing, so it never blocks the work's start; `finish` is refused until it passes, with the same code and a fix naming the approvers. It is passed as a human gate is (decision 68): by a principal it names, else a project admin, only from the session `cli`.

**When it is worth having.** Where the host's branch protection requires a review, `landed` already means reviewed, and a second review in Aries is ceremony. Review is for what the host does not see: a design with no code, a decision, an operations task, or a host with no required reviews. So it is off by default and set per type. Question 7.

**Approval before start.** A profile's `approve: {approvers: [...]}` makes, in the create's write, a human gate that blocks the new issue, so that it and every descendant stay out of `ready` until a person passes it (§20.3): an epic's plan approved before its children start (Bearings §3.13). Its `gate.pending` goes to the approvers as any gate's.

**Who is the author.** Until agents are principals of their own (decision 8; enterprise review E2), an agent working under its person's key is that person, so "not the author" cannot be enforced between a person and their agents; the `cli` session is the speed bump, as for every human gate. When E2 lands, a profile gains `review.not_author: true`, refusing a pass by the principal, or the owner of the agent principal, that holds or last held the issue.

### 21.7 The close-out checklist

A profile's `closeout` lines are what the harness does when work ends: tear down what it started, archive the session, and, for an epic, prune worktrees and merged branches and put the main checkout back on `main` (the memory's step 8 and the issue's comment of 16 Oct 2026). Aries sees no worktree, container or session, so it cannot check any of it.

| Option | For | Against |
|---|---|---|
| **A. Shown, not tracked (recommended)** | No self-attestation dressed as a check; no schema token; a result field, so no protocol change | Nothing records that it was done |
| B. Ticked like acceptance items | A record | The agent ticks what nothing can verify; `finish` gains an argument (MCP tokens, a protocol bump) |

- **Where it is shown.** In the results of `handoff` with state `done` and of `finish`, as `closeout`, which `aries mcp` puts as a short list after the result line, within the result's 2,000-token cap and dropped last; in the inbox item of a closing pass (§21.5), since the session that did the work may be gone by then; and for an epic, in the result of the `finish` or `close` that closes it, and in `digest` once its last child closed.
- **Hooks.** A harness with a session-end hook may run its own close-out script; `aries setup` does not write one, since the steps are the team's and their commands differ by machine.

### 21.8 When CI cannot reach the server

`landed` is reported by a job running `aries` after the merge (§20.4, decision 67). A server on a laptop, as the dogfood server is, or behind a VPN or IAP a hosted runner cannot use, never hears from it, and under §21.5's option 1 nothing it governs would close.

| Option | What it takes | What a pass is worth |
|---|---|---|
| **1. `aries land`, on a person's machine (recommended for one maintainer)** | A new command, over the existing `gate.pass`: after `git pull` on the default branch, it reads each commit since the last it reported (kept in the data directory, per project), takes the issue from the `Aries:` trailer or a merge commit's subject as `--from-commit` does, and reports `landed` with the commit, from the session `cli`. The workflow's `ci` names the person's principal. The coordinating session runs it at step 7, or a `post-merge` hook does, which `aries setup` may offer and never writes unasked | What the person's checkout saw on the default branch; as much as their key |
| 2. A self-hosted runner, or a tunnel, that reaches the server | docs/ci.md's workflow on a runner inside the network, or with Tailscale, WireGuard or IAP (decision 65) | As G1's |
| 3. A relay that polls the host's API | A client process holding a host token, built outside ariesd if asked (§20.4, option 3) | As the relay's key; deferred |
| 4. No required checks | `checks: []`: the default items stay, and the memory's step 7 is a person's again | Nothing beyond today's |

`aries land` asks the server nothing new: a pass of a closed gate is a no-op, so running it twice, or on two machines, is harmless; a commit that names no issue is skipped with a note (`--optional`'s rule); a report from it carries no `run`, so it is taken in the order it arrives (§20.15, runs out of order). `build`, which only CI runs, cannot be reported this way; a team in this case leaves `build` out of `checks` and keeps "CI green on the PR" an item the agent ticks.

### 21.9 Many projects, and an organization's default

**Within one server,** each project's workflow is its own row (§21.3, A), set by that project's admins; a server admin may set any. A server of one project, with `admins:` in the settings, works the same way.

**An organization's default.** Bearings decision 31 (§3.15) makes the server the organization. A server admin applies a default for every project, `aries admin workflow set --server FILE`, kept in the control store (a `settings` table in a new control migration, with `workflow.set` in the control log and the audit stream) and held in the control index the daemon reads at no cost (§15.1, As built), as capabilities are.

**How it cascades.** The effective profile of a type is computed by the daemon at create, from the server's default and the project's own:

| In the server's default | In the project's |
|---|---|
| An item, check, review or approval marked `required: true` | Kept; the project cannot remove it. An apply that would is refused `invalid`, naming the line |
| Anything else | A default the project may replace by naming the type |
| Close-out lines | Shown before the project's |

So the server's default is a **floor**, and a project may only add to it: the rule Bearings §3.18 gives `policy.apply`, "refusing a child looser than its parent". The create's event records both revs, `workflow: {server, project, type}`.

**Staying compatible with what is to come.**
- **Organization memory** (aries-iakie56e) is guidance agents read; the workflow is what the server enforces. They should share their addressing: a scope that is the server (organization) today, a portfolio later (Bearings §3.15's `scope_kind` and `scope`), a project last. Recommended: whichever is built first defines that scope key in the control store, and the other uses it; a server-level workflow and organization memory are two rows at the same scope.
- **Portfolios** add one layer between server and project with the same floor rule, when §3.15 is built; nothing here changes.
- **Policy as code** (Bearings §3.18). Its `projects/NAME.toml` lists "gates per project; reviewer constraints" among structural policy. This document is that part: Bearings' `policy-apply` principal calls the same `workflow.set`, and the second signer and the recorded commit are Bearings' additions on top, not a second store. Recommended: Bearings P0 builds on `workflow.set` and its floor check rather than beside them.
- **Roles** (enterprise review E6). Today a project admin sets the project's workflow; when E6 brings narrower roles, a `policy` role (§3.18's `policy_owner`) may set it without the rest of a project admin's rights.
- **Autonomy windows** (E13) bound what an agent may do unattended; a workflow says how any work must end. A window may later name a stricter workflow for the work done in it; nothing here assumes one.

### 21.10 What changes on the server

- **Ops.** `workflow.set` (project and server admins; the document, the expected rev, and `scope: project|server`) and `workflow` (a read of the project's document, the server's, and the effective profiles, by any member, so that `prime` can summarize it). Both are new ops, a protocol change under rule 10: **from v0.5.0 on, one bump** (`Proto` and `ProtoMax` 9, `ProtoMin` 8, release-versioning); a slice that merges before v0.5.0 is tagged joins 8 as decision 78 allows. Nothing else is an argument: defaults, required checks, gates made by a report, and the closing pass are the server's behavior on existing ops, so a protocol 8 client is held to the workflow as a 9 one is.
- **Results** (no bump; clients ignore unknown fields): `create`'s `items`; `show`'s `workflow` (rev and type) and `checks` with the required ones marked; `handoff`'s and `finish`'s `closeout`; `digest`'s and `prime`'s `landing`, the issues awaiting landing.
- **Migrations.** Project set: none; the `settings` table exists. Control set: `settings`, for the server's default (W4).
- **Events** (rule 7): `workflow.set`, target `setting:workflow` (no issue id matches it, so the board's push does not carry it), with before and after; in the control log, the same for the server's default. `issue.create` and an `update` of type gain `workflow` in their after state. The report that makes a gate records `gate.add` before its `gate.pass` or `gate.fail`; a bound item's tick is `acceptance.tick` by the reporter; the closing pass is `issue.close` with `reason: landed`; a revert's is `issue.reopen` with `reason: check failed`.
- **Inbox:** `gate.passed` and `gate.failed` to the subject's assignee and last handoff's author, as G1's are; no new kind.
- **Errors:** `acceptance` for a `finish` or `close` held by a required check or review, with its fix; `invalid` for a setting refused at set, naming the key and line.
- **Limits** (rule 18), each a `limits:` setting with a row in `docs/server.md`:

  | Limit | Default |
  |---|---|
  | `workflow_bytes` (one document) | 16 KiB, at most 64 KiB |
  | `workflow_items_per_type` | 20, at most 100 |
  | `workflow_checks_per_type` | 5, at most `gate_checks` |
  | `workflow_closeout_per_type` | 20, at most 100 |

  A review's or approval's approvers are capped by `approvers_per_gate`, and the items an issue ends with by `acceptance_items_per_issue` (200): a `create` whose own items and the defaults together pass it is refused `invalid`, naming both counts.
- **Telemetry and logs** (§16.4): the log event `workflow.set` (fields: project, by, rev, scope; no text); `aries.gates` already counts the gates reports make. A workflow's lines are a person's text and go on no metric or span (rule 2).
- **Offline** (§18.3):

  | Queued | Never queued |
  |---|---|
  | `create`, which gets the defaults in force when it is replayed, not when it was queued: the replay is the write. The queued answer says so ("workflow applies when sent"). `handoff`. `finish`, whose replay a required check may refuse, which then lands in the refused list with the fix to hand off | `workflow.set` (an admin's act, as every admin op); `gate.*`, as §20.9 has it, so `aries land` refuses offline and remembers nothing it did not report |

  The cache keeps `workflow` with `prime`'s answer, so an offline `prime` shows the summary it last saw, saying so.
- **MCP cost:** no tool and no argument, so **0 schema tokens** against the 3,000 (the 25 tools measure about 2,773 today). Results: `prime` gains one line, about 25 tokens of its 1,500 (`workflow rev 4: task, bug, feature: 4 items, landed; epic: approval`); the close-out list and the awaiting-landing lines are dropped last under their results' caps. The refusal's fix is reworded as the next call by `internal/mcpserver/errors.go`, as every refusal is.
- **The pinned memory stays** for what no server can hold: branch names, worktrees, test first, never merge. It shrinks to those steps once W1 is built.

### 21.11 Plan

| Slice | Delivers | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| W0 | The setting: `workflow.set` and `workflow`, `aries admin workflow` set, show and diff, `aries workflow check`, validation, `workflow.set` events, the caps; default items at `create` and `finish`'s discovered issues, and on a change of type; `prime`'s line; docs/concepts.md and docs/server.md | An agent's `create` through MCP, and a protocol 8 client's, gets the profile's items; a later set changes no existing issue; the event names the rev; an agent cannot remove an item except by waiving; a set by a member is refused `forbidden`, and over MCP there is no way to try |
| W1 | Required checks: the gate made by the first report, `finish` and `close` refused with the fix, items bound to checks, the closing pass, reopen on revert, awaiting landing in `digest` and `prime` | e2e: an issue whose profile requires `landed` is refused at `finish`; a CI principal's `--from-commit` pass with no gate added closes it and tells its assignee; a principal the workflow does not name is refused; a failure after reopens it. The soak's replay holds every `issue.close` of such an issue to a pass |
| W2 | `aries land`, its state per project, the optional `post-merge` hook in `aries setup`; docs/ci.md's section for a server CI cannot reach | Two runs of `aries land` over the same commits report each once and change nothing the second time; a commit naming no issue is skipped with a note; offline it reports nothing and remembers nothing |
| W3 | Review and approval per type | An epic created under `approve` keeps its children out of `ready` until an approver passes from `cli`; an issue under `review` cannot finish until passed; MCP within budget, unchanged |
| W4 | Close-out lines in `handoff`, `finish`, the closing pass's inbox item and an epic's close | Golden-tested results, within their caps |
| W5 | The server's default with the floor rule, in the control store, with organization memory's scope key | A project's apply that loosens a required line is refused, naming it; a server default applies to every project's next `create`; both revs on the event |

W0 alone replaces the hand-typed items. W1 with W2 is what makes "closed" mean "landed" for this repository, whose server CI cannot reach. W3 and W4 are small; W5 waits for organization memory so the two share a scope.

### 21.12 Decisions for the maintainer

**Decided 16 Oct 2026** (decision 95, §14): the maintainer accepted all fourteen as recommended. Asked: "Accept all 14 as recommended". The questions as they were put follow.

1. **The workflow is a project setting,** a row of the project's existing `settings` table, set as a whole document. Recommended (§21.3, A). Cost: the server's default lives in the control store, apart.
2. **It is a file in the repository, applied by `aries admin workflow set`,** with `diff` and `check`, never over MCP. Recommended. Cost: an apply is a person's step after the pull request merges, until Bearings' `policy-apply` does it.
3. **Defaults are appended to the acceptance text at create,** and on a change of type the new profile's are added and the old ones kept; an item leaves only by waiver. Recommended (§21.4, A). Cost: the text holds items its creator did not type.
4. **A create queued offline gets the defaults in force at its replay.** Recommended. Cost: the queued answer cannot list them.
5. **Close means landed:** `finish` and `close` are refused while a required check has not passed, the passing report closes the issue, and a later failure reopens it. Recommended (§21.5, option 1). Cost: an issue waits in progress with no holder until it lands; a team must have a way to report.
6. **Items bound to a check are ticked by its pass;** an agent may still tick them too. Recommended, as the lighter rule. The alternative, only the check ticks a bound item, is stricter and refuses an agent's tick with a fix.
7. **Review before close is per type and off by default; approval before start is per type, for epics.** Each is a human gate passed as decision 68 says. Recommended. Cost: "not the author" waits for agent identities (E2).
8. **The close-out checklist is shown, not tracked.** Recommended (§21.7, A).
9. **Project admins and server admins set it; a `policy` role later, with E6.** Recommended.
10. **The server's default is a floor** that a project may add to and not loosen, in the control store, built with organization memory and sharing its scope. Recommended (§21.9). Bearings P0 then builds on `workflow.set`.
11. **For a server CI cannot reach, `aries land`** run by a named person after pulling the default branch; a self-hosted runner or a tunnel documented; `checks: []` as the fallback. Recommended (§21.8, option 1).
12. **One protocol bump** for W0's two ops, `ProtoMin` kept at 8; W1 to W4 add only behavior and result fields. Recommended.
13. **Read-only work gets a type** whose profile has no pull-request items (this repository would use `chore`), not an exemption label, which an agent could add to escape the workflow. Recommended. Cost: filing such work with the right type matters.
14. **Not retroactive:** a workflow applies to issues created after it is set. Recommended. The v0.5.0 issues keep the items typed onto them; a one-time `aries admin workflow apply --open`, recorded issue by issue, can be built if wanted.

### 21.13 What this does not solve

- **Aries sees no branch, test run or pull request.** An item such as "Tests run as the tiers require" is the agent's word when it ticks it; only bound checks are reported by something else.
- **A check is as good as the key that reports it** (§20.13). From a laptop, `landed` means the maintainer's checkout saw the commit on the default branch.
- **A person and their agents are one principal** until E2, so a review or approval from `cli` is a speed bump, not separation of duties.
- **Never merging is the host's rule** (branch protection, a merge queue); Aries records what landed, not who pressed the button.
- **The close-out steps are not checked.** A stack left running is found by `docker compose ls`, not by Aries.

### 21.14 Not tested

Everything: this is a design. In particular: what `create` costs when it reads the setting and, under W5, the control index; `digest`'s awaiting-landing query at 50,000 issues; and whether a `post-merge` hook is reliable across the harnesses' worktrees, which share one repository's hooks.

### 21.15 As built

**As built (W0, the setting and default items at create, 16 Oct 2026).** Slice W0 of §21.11 (aries-bgknkrxt). Where the build differs from §21.3, §21.4 and §21.10, or settles what they left open:
- **The row.** The `settings` row `workflow` (project migration 0001's table; no migration), its `value` the document as JSON in the form `proto.ParseWorkflow` gives, its `rev` the workflow's, its `source` `workflow.set`. Who set it and when are read from the last `workflow.set` event, the table having no column for them. A set of the document in force writes nothing and answers `changed: false` at the same rev, so applying the same file twice is harmless. `--rev N` refuses a set over another rev with `conflict`; without it the set applies over whatever is there, as a person's act at a terminal.
- **Who.** An admin (`Store.IsAdmin`: `admins:`, and in a server of many projects a project admin or a server admin) **from the session `cli` only**: a set from any other session, an agent's or a CI job's, is refused `forbidden` (`store.InPersonError`), with a fix to run it in one's own terminal, as a human gate's pass is (decision 68). §21.3 did not say; without it an agent under its person's key could change the workflow it works under by running `aries admin workflow set` in its shell, which §21.2 rules out. A member is refused `forbidden`, with a fix to ask a project admin. `workflow`, the read, is open to every member and to sign-ins of scope `mcp` and `ci`; `workflow.set` to a key or a sign-in of scope `cli` alone.
- **The document.** `proto.ParseWorkflow` decodes it key by key, so a refusal names the key, as `types.bug.acceptence: unknown key`, rather than a line of the YAML, which the server never sees; `aries` runs the same parse on the file before it sends it. Beyond §21.3's checks: a list names a principal or a check once; an item's text is kept with its whitespace collapsed, as an item is read, and refused when `- TEXT` would not read back as that one item, so `[x] …`, which would tick itself, cannot be a default; a line is at most 500 bytes. **An item bound to a check must name a check of its profile's `checks`**, as §21.3's rule has it; §21.3's own example binds `CI green on the PR` to `build` without requiring `build`, which this refuses. This repository's `.aries/workflow.yaml` follows §21.8 instead: `build` is no check, since CI cannot report it to this project's server, and its item is the agent's to tick; `ci` names the maintainer, `ed`, who reports `landed` (W2), not a CI principal. `scope: server` is refused `invalid` until W5. Members are checked through `Options.IsMember`, which a server of many projects answers from the control index; a server of one project knows its members only by their keys, and takes any principal name.
- **Limits** as §21.10 has them, `workflow_checks_per_type` at most the configured `checks_per_issue`. The document's size is measured as the server keeps it, compact JSON, not as the file holds it.
- **Defaults.** In the write of `create`, of each discovered issue of `finish` (live or replayed), and of an `update` that changes the type of an issue that is not closed: the items of the profile the type gets (its own, else `default`) that the text lacks, compared as `acceptance_state` keys them, are appended as `- TEXT` lines, after the creator's text and in the profile's order. Text that holds no Markdown list, which is one item, becomes the list's first entry, so that it stays an item. A template, a gate and `ariesd import-bd` take none. An update that changes the type and the text applies the new profile to the new text. The cap of §21.10 applies to the result, and its refusal names both counts.
- **Recorded.** `issue.create`'s after state is the issue with `workflow: {rev, type}`, `type` being the profile applied (`default` or a type), or `{rev}` alone when a workflow is set and the type gets no profile; an `update` of type records the same in its after state. Nothing is recorded where no workflow was ever set. §21.4's copy of the profile's required checks into the issue's `metadata` is not made: `metadata` is the client's to replace, and the log already holds what W1 needs, the rev and profile on the issue's event and every rev's document on the `workflow.set` events.
- **Results.** `create`'s `items`, which the CLI prints on stderr; the MCP `create` tool still answers `{id, rev}`, and an agent sees the items at `start` and `show`. `workflow` answers the rev, the document, who set it and when, and `effective`, the profile of each type that gets one. `prime` adds `workflow`, its one line, from `proto.WorkflowResult.Summary`, which holds only type and check names and counts, so it is shown among Aries's own lines, above the data fence; a server without the op leaves it out, and the cached prime keeps the line it last had.
- **Protocol.** `workflow.set` and `workflow` join unreleased protocol 8 with no bump, as §21.10 allows for a slice that merges before v0.5.0 is tagged, and rule 10 as decision 93 left it: decision 95's one bump applies to changes from v0.5.0 on. `Proto`, `ProtoMin` and `ProtoMax` stay 8. A server built from main before W0 refuses both as unknown ops; the defaults apply to a create from any client, which sends nothing new.
- **Offline.** `workflow.set` is never queued (`outbox.Unqueued`). A queued `create` says, in the CLI's line and the MCP's, that the workflow applies when it is sent.
- **MCP.** No tool and no argument: 0 schema tokens. `prime`'s line is about 25 tokens.
- **Not built in W0:** the log event `workflow.set` and its fields (§21.10); the server's default (W5); and the enforcement of `checks`, `review`, `approve` and `closeout`, which W0 checks at set, keeps and shows (W1, W3, W4). Not measured: what reading the setting adds to a `create`, one more indexed read in its write.

**As built (W2, `aries land`, 16 Oct 2026).** Slice W2 of §21.11 (aries-uwubz3jj), on `main` before W1, so that it works with a gate added by hand and with one a report makes. Where the build differs from §21.8 or settles what it left open:
- **The command.** `aries land [--from COMMIT] [--branch NAME] [--dry-run] [--hook]`, a CLI command only (rule 12), no op and no protocol change: it calls `gate.pass` with `check: landed`, the commit as `sha`, and the issue from `gitx.IssueOfMessage`, which `gate pass --from-commit` now uses too. It sets its session to `cli`, whatever the environment says, as §21.8 has it, because the gate's principal is a person's. A merge commit's branch is sent as `branch`, and the server reads the issue from it.
- **State.** `landed`, a file of the server-and-project directory of the data directory (`offline.Local.Landed`, `SetLanded`; the rules of the directory apply: the user's own, 0600), holding one commit hash. It is written after each commit reported or skipped, never for one that did not reach the server, so a run that fails midway resumes at the commit that failed. It is per project, not per checkout: a second checkout of the same repository reports from the same place.
- **What it reads.** The commits of HEAD's first-parent line after the kept one, oldest first, at most 500 (`gitx.Since`). With none kept and no `--from`, HEAD alone, with a note, so that the first run in a long history reports no backlog, which a report with W1 could close. A kept commit the repository no longer has is `invalid`, with the fix `--from COMMIT`.
- **Where it runs.** On the default branch only (origin's HEAD, else `main` or `master`; `--branch` names it), refused elsewhere with the fix: what another branch holds is not merged.
- **Skipped, with a note on stderr:** a commit that names no issue, and one the server answers `not_found` to (no `landed` gate, before W1; an issue whose type requires none, after). Any other refusal stops the run at that commit.
- **The hook.** `aries setup post-merge [--write|--check|--remove]` is no agent's: it finds git's hooks directory (`gitx.HookPath`: the worktrees' shared one, or `core.hooksPath`), prints the script by default, writes only with `--write`, and only where there is no hook; it never replaces one of someone else's, and removes only a file that holds nothing but its own line. `land --hook` prints nothing off the default branch and turns any error into one line on stderr, exit 0, as `prime --hook` does.
- **Tests.** e2e `TestLand` and `TestLandRefusedKeepsItsPlace` with a git repository made in the test: a first run, a rerun that changes nothing, a dry run, a merge commit and a commit naming no issue or no gate, off the default branch, the server away and back, the hook; against gates added by hand (the report that creates its gate is W1's).

**As built (W1, close means landed, 17 Oct 2026).** Slice W1 of §21.11 (aries-g72emn5j), on W0. Where the build differs from §21.5 and §21.10, or settles what they left open:
- **What an issue requires** is read in each write that needs it from the issue's own events (`store/landed.go`): the workflow stamps of its `issue.create` and of each `update` of type, as W0 records them, and for each stamp's rev the document on the `workflow.set` event that made it. Every profile stamped on the issue counts, so a change of type adds the new profile's checks and keeps the old ones, as it does items (§21.4); the check's `ci` principals are those of the latest stamp that requires it. Nothing is copied into `metadata` (W0's note). An issue made before any workflow, or of a type whose profile names no checks, requires nothing.
- **The gate made by the first report** is made in the report's write, through the same code as `gate add` (`insertGate`), with `gate.add` before the report's event, its `by` the workflow's `ci`, its creator the reporter, holding nothing; `checks_per_issue`, `approvers_per_gate` and `open_gates_per_project` apply as to any gate. A principal it does not name is refused (`forbidden`, not approver) and the write is undone, gate and all. A workflow whose `ci` is empty makes no gate: the report is refused `invalid`, saying a project admin names the principals under `ci`. A report by gate id, or for a check the issue does not require, still needs a gate someone added, as in G1.
- **The refusal.** `finish` and `close` while a required check's gate has not passed or been waived are refused with `store.AcceptanceError`, which gains `Checks`, each with its gate and last report; code `acceptance`, whose message names the checks (`waits for check landed (failed at 0123abcd0123), which closes it when it passes`) and the open items with them, and whose fix begins `hand it off` (`proto.FixLand`): `aries handoff ID --state done` with the pull request's link. `aries mcp` words it as the next call, without `accept`, which is no tool: hand off now, and finish with the items ticked if the inbox later says an item is left. A refused `finish` writes nothing, its ticks and discovered issues included. `close --force` by an admin closes it, recording `checks_overridden` beside `acceptance_overridden`.
- **The closing pass** runs when a pass changed the gate (a pass of a passed gate, or a stale run, does nothing) and the subject is not closed: the bound items are ticked with `acceptance.tick` events by the reporter; then, with every required check passed or waived, no item open and no open gate holding the subject, `closeTx` closes it, reason `landed`, ending the claim and telling its holder (`claim.lost`), as any close by another does. Its `gate.passed` goes to the assignee and the last handoff's author, each once, saying it closed; with an item open or a gate holding it, the same item names what is left. It replaces, for those principals, G1's "passed after a failure" item. A pass while another required check waits tells nobody beyond G1's items.
- **The revert.** A failure of a gate that had passed unticks the bound items (`acceptance.untick`) and, when the subject is closed, reopens it in the same write: `issue.reopen` by the reporter with `reason: check failed` in its after state. Its assignee hears of it from G1's `gate.failed`, which already goes to the subject's holder and assignee. A failure of a gate that had not passed changes no item.
- **Who may change whose issue.** The closing pass, the bound items' ticks and the reopen change an issue whoever holds it, with no `guard` and no `admin.override`: they are the report of the principal the gate names. AGENTS.md rule 5 names this exemption beside `ReapClaims`.
- **Awaiting landing.** `Dates` and `Digest` gain `Landing`: issues in progress whose last handoff has state `done` and that wait for a required check, longest waiting first, `At` the handoff's time, `By` the assignee, `Note` the checks as the refusal names them. The `dates` and `digest` results gain `landing` and `totals.landing` (result fields, no bump); `prime` lists at most three, as `awaiting landing`, and `aries digest` and the `digest` tool a section. A query reads at most 500 candidates, each with its events and gates, and sets `truncated` past them. Not measured at 50,000 issues (§21.14).
- **Protocol.** No new op and no new argument: W1 is behavior on `finish`, `close`, `gate.pass` and `gate.fail`, and result fields, so `Proto`, `ProtoMin` and `ProtoMax` stay 8, and a protocol 8 client is held to it whatever it sends.
- **Offline.** Nothing new: a queued `finish` that a required check refuses when it is replayed goes to the refused list with the server's code and fix, as any refusal does (`TestLandedReplayedFinishRefused`).
- **The soak.** The first project's workflow requires `landed` of a bug; the sessions file some issues as bugs, and root reports `landed` on them from a CI job's session, passing and now and then failing. The replay holds every `issue.close` of an issue whose recorded workflow requires a check to a pass of it before, or to an admin's force recording it overridden; a close with reason `landed` to a pass of its check in the same transaction; a reopen for a failed check to a failure in its transaction; and every report to a principal its gate names.
- **With W2.** W2 merged first, so `aries land` already reports `landed` from the session `cli`; with W1 its report makes the gate of an issue that requires `landed`, and its pass is the closing pass. A commit whose issue requires no `landed` is still answered `not_found` and skipped, as W2 has it.
- **Not built in W1:** the log event `workflow.set` (still W0's note), and review, approval and close-out (W3, W4). Not measured: what reading an issue's events and gates adds to each `finish`, `close` and report.

**As built (W3, review and approval, 17 Oct 2026).** Slice W3 of §21.11 (aries-l2dz6gav), on W1. Where the build differs from §21.6, or settles what it left open:
- **Where the gates are made.** In the write of `create` and of each discovered issue of `finish`, after the issue exists (`Store.profileGates`, `store/review.go`), from the workflow in force then: an `approve` gate first, then a `review` gate, each through `insertGate`, so `gate.add`, `gate.pending` to the approvers, `approvers_per_gate` and `open_gates_per_project` apply as to any gate; a create past the limit is refused whole. A template, `ariesd import-bd` and a change of type make none: a change of type adds the new profile's items and checks (W0, W1) but not its gates, so a review or approval is a promise made at create. Nothing is copied into `metadata`; the gate is the record.
- **The approval** is `AddGate` with `blocks` the new issue, so G-series holds apply unchanged: the issue and its descendants are out of `ready`, a start is refused `held`, and `blocked` shows the gate.
- **The review** is a human gate with the issue as its `subject` (a column only ci gates used) and no `blocks` edge, so it holds nothing. `reviewWaits` reads the open ones; `closeTx` refuses `finish` and `close` with an `AcceptanceError` that gains `Reviews` (gate and approvers), code `acceptance`, fix `proto.FixReview` (`ask a person to review`), which `aries mcp` words as: do not finish again, tell the user, never run `aries gate`. A forced close by an admin records `reviews_overridden`. A waive by an admin passes it too, as for any gate. A review gate's own pass or waive tells the subject's holder and assignee (`gate.passed`: finish it when its items are met).
- **With W1.** The closing pass of a required check does not close an issue whose review is open; its inbox item says it waits for the review, then to finish. After the review passes the issue is finished by its agent as usual; the review's pass does not itself close it.
- **Passing** is decision 68's, unchanged: the approvers the profile names, else an admin other than the gate's creator, only from the session `cli`. A profile naming no approver leaves the pass to the project's admins. `not_author` waits for E2, as §21.6 says.
- **Protocol.** None: no op, no argument and no result field changes; `Proto`, `ProtoMin` and `ProtoMax` stay 8, and no bump is needed. A protocol 8 client is held to a review and an approval whatever it sends.
- **MCP.** No tool and no schema token: 0 added; the reworded refusal is an error string.
- **Tests.** `store/review_test.go` (table-driven over pass and waive), the server's error table, the MCP reword table, and e2e `TestReviewAndApproval`.
- **Not built in W3:** the review's gate in `digest`'s *awaiting landing*, which counts required checks alone. Not measured: what the extra gate query adds to each `finish` and `close`.

**As built (W4, the close-out checklist, 17 Oct 2026).** Slice W4 of §21.11 (aries-cqn3i4a5), on W1. It replaces step 8 of the memory `workflow`. Where the build differs from §21.7 and §21.10, or settles what they left open:
- **What is shown** is the `closeout` lines of every profile stamped on the issue, in the order stamped, each once, read from its own log as W1 reads its checks (`store.Closeout`, `closeoutLines`): a later set of the workflow changes nothing, and a change of type adds the new profile's lines. Read after the write, outside it: a failed read is logged and leaves the result without the lines, since the write is made.
- **Results.** `FinishResult.Closeout` for every `finish` that applied (not a lapsed one, nor one kept as a comment); `HandoffResult.Closeout` for a `handoff` whose state is `done`; `CloseResult` (a `WriteResult` and `Closeout`) for a `close` of an issue whose type is `epic`. No new op and no argument, so `Proto`, `ProtoMin` and `ProtoMax` stay 8 and a client that does not know the field ignores it (protocol 8 clients are unchanged). A type that gets no profile, an issue made before any workflow and a profile with no lines have none, and the field is omitted.
- **MCP.** `finish`, `handoff` and `close` answer their JSON result line as before and, when there are lines, a second text block `closeout:` and a `- line` each (`mcpserver/closeout.go`). It is not in the JSON: the result stays the document it was, and the schema set is unchanged (0 tokens). The block fits what the cap leaves after the result and room for the inbox notice (40 tokens): it is cut by whole lines, the first always kept, ending in `- … N more`, so the checklist gives way before the result does. A checklist is at most 20 lines of 500 bytes by default, about 3,300 tokens, so the cut can bite; golden tests hold a result with 20 long lines under 2,000 tokens.
- **CLI.** `aries handoff`, `finish` and `close` print `closeout:` and `  - line` each after the `ID rev N` line; `--json` has the `closeout` array.
- **The closing pass** sends a second `gate.passed` item to the same people, after the one that says the issue closed: `close-out of ID: line; line`. Its own item, because an inbox item holds 200 bytes (`InboxBodyMax`) and the checklist would be cut from the first one; the cut still applies to the second, and `finish`'s result and `aries workflow` hold the lines whole. A pass that leaves an item open or a gate holding the issue sends none.
- **Digest.** `Digest.Closeout` (store), `DigestResult.Closeout` and `Totals.Closeout`, in `aries digest` as *epics to close out* and in the `digest` tool as `closeout`: epics not closed that have at least one child, every child closed, and lines in their profiles, oldest update first, `Note` the lines joined by `; ` (cut at 300 bytes in MCP). It filters by `by` (the assignee) and `label` as `landing` does, reads at most 500 candidates, and has no window: an epic stays listed until it closes.
- **Not built:** the epic's lines only at the close that closes it and in digest; a `finish` of a child does not show its parent's. Hooks are the harness's own (`aries setup` writes none). Nothing records that a line was done (option B was not chosen).

**As built (aries-poekpmjc, an agent settles its items at handoff, 17 Oct 2026).** A bug found on v0.5.0's first day: over MCP the only way to tick an acceptance item was `finish`, which an agent under a required `landed` check cannot call before the merge, so the closing pass (§21.4) found the met items open and closed nothing; four issues had to be finished by hand. Of the three fixes the issue offered, (a) was built:
- **Why (a).** `handoff` with state `done` takes `ticked` and `waived`, as `finish` does. It is the call an agent already makes at that moment, the smallest change (two schema fields; `accept` as a tool would add a tool's description and an argument set, and (c), a refused `finish` that records its ticks, makes a refusal write).
- **Where.** `HandoffNote.Accept`, applied by `Store.handoff` with `applyAcceptance` after the guard and before the note, in the handoff's own write, so the items' events (`acceptance.tick`, `acceptance.waive`) precede the handoff comment. Ticks with any state but `done`, or on a closed issue, are refused (`invalid`; `StateError`). A handoff kept as a comment because the issue moved on (a replay, §18.6) ticks nothing.
- **Protocol.** `HandoffArgs` gains `ticked` and `waived`: a new argument under rule 10, the first change since v0.5.0. `Proto` and `ProtoMax` are 9, `ProtoMin` stays 8, and a protocol 8 client, which sends neither, is served as before (`TestProtocol8ClientHandsOff`). A new client against a v0.5.0 server, which accepts 8 alone, is refused at the handshake with code `version` and told to have the server upgraded (`TestCheckProtoBothDirections`); `TestReleasedClients` gained the v0.5.0 client.
- **MCP.** `handoff` gains `ticked` and `waived`, validated like `finish`'s; the refusal for a required check now tells the agent to hand off with them.
- **CLI.** Unchanged: `aries handoff` has no tick flag, `aries accept` does that.
- **Tests.** `store.TestHandoffTicksLetLandedClose`, `TestHandoffWaivesItem`, `TestHandoffTicksNeedDone`, and e2e `TestHandoffTicksLand`.

## 22. Briefs, stored on the issue they start

Proposed and built 23 Oct 2026 (aries-2qx4mlm2). Asked by the maintainer, 17 Oct 2026: the brief a session is started with lived only in the harness's local transcript, deleted after 30 days, while Aries kept everything else of the run (claim, handoff, landed, gates, usage). It is wanted for two readers. The productivity metrics compare outcomes by brief, model and effort (productivity-metrics.md, M36), which needs the brief joined to the claim's session. And the merge rule ([merges.md](merges.md) §6, decision 5) holds every pull request whose class needs the opener, because the record it reads, a `brief:` comment, is any member's to write: slice M4 reads the stored record instead.

### 22.1 How the brief reaches the store

The issue named three ways. **Chosen: the starter records it, through an op of its own, `brief`, CLI only.**

- **An argument to `start`** is written by the session that starts the issue: the worker would record its own brief, which is the one thing the opener must not be. A worker does not reliably know its model and effort, nor see its brief as sent (a harness takes background apart). It would also grow the MCP schema set (rule 12) for a field agents should not fill.
- **The harness hook reading the first prompt**, as `internal/capture` reads usage, reads a file in the worker's own home, by the worker's own process: an agent can change it before the hook runs. It is one harness's format (Claude Code's transcript), and a Desktop session's transcript puts the coordinator's background apart from its brief.
- **The starter** already has all of it: `aries dispatch brief` renders the brief and names the harness, model, effort, session and template, and the dispatcher renders every run's. Recording it there keeps the brief verbatim (the hash `aries outcome` reads is of the text sent) and makes the recorder someone other than the worker. It builds on the record the memory `briefs` fixed (§22.2), so no reader changes.

A plain `comment` would have done as a transport, but the kind is what the merge rule needs: a comment that merely begins `brief:` must not be mistaken for a recorded one. So `brief` is an op, which raises the protocol to 11 (rule 10): `Proto` and `ProtoMax` are 11, `ProtoMin` 10, v0.7.0's. A protocol 10 client sends no brief and is served as before; a new client against a v0.7.0 server is refused at the handshake.

### 22.2 The record

A brief is a comment of kind `brief`, whose body is the record the memory `briefs` fixed, built by the server from the fields (`internal/brief`'s `Record.Line`): the line `brief: harness=H model=M effort=E session=S template=T`, a blank line, then the brief verbatim. So `comments`, `aries outcome` and `aries prs merges` read it as they read the comment today, and its author and session are the connection's: the principal from the key or sign-in, never an argument (rule 12), and the session the recorder runs in. A table beside it, `briefs` (migration 0004), keeps the fields, the SHA-256 of the brief (`brief.Hash`), and the claim it is joined to (§22.3). The comment's `comment.add` event carries the fields and the hash, as a handoff's carries its fields.

- **Fields.** `harness`, `model`, `effort`, `session` and `template` are each one word, at most 128 bytes (`session` 255), with no space, so that the line parses field by field; `effort` is `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max` or `default`. `harness`, `model` and `template` are required.
- **The text** is required, held to safetext as a comment is (newlines and tabs only), capped at `brief_bytes` under `limits:` (default 32,768, at most 64,000, which leaves room in a comment for the line), and refused if `internal/secretscan` finds what looks like a credential in it or in a field, naming the field and the kind, never the text. Each refusal is `invalid` with a Fix.
- **Not telemetry.** The op's span carries its name and result code, as every op's does; no text, model or effort is an attribute, so the priced-model rule of `aries.usage.tokens` (§16) is not needed.

### 22.3 Joined to the claim

A brief is recorded before the session it starts claims the issue, and is joined to the claim that session takes:

- **Pending.** A brief recorded while no live claim of another session is on the issue waits. A second brief recorded while one waits **replaces** it: the first stays as history and is never joined, so a starter who got it wrong records again.
- **Joined at start.** A `start` that takes a new claim (a new epoch) joins the waiting brief to it, in the same write, with an event `brief.bind` naming the comment, the claim's principal, session and epoch. A start that only renews joins nothing.
- **Joined at once.** A brief recorded while another session holds a live claim with no brief, and naming that session (`session=` is the Aries session the run claims under, as the dispatcher's is), is joined to that claim at once: the dispatcher claims before it launches (decision 96). A Desktop session's `local_…` id is never its Aries session, so its brief waits for its start.
- **One brief per claim.** A brief naming a claim that has one already is refused, with a Fix to record the next brief before the next start.
- **Never one's own.** A brief recorded by the session that holds the issue's live claim is refused (`invalid`): a session never briefs itself.

`show` returns the issue's latest brief: its fields, its hash, who recorded it from which session, and its claim, with the text under `--full` (the CLI) and never over MCP, whose `show` points to `comments` for it. `history` returns the `comment.add` and `brief.bind` events, and `comments` the whole comment. Everyone who may read the issue may read its brief, as with any comment.

### 22.4 What the merge rule may trust

The record tells M4 three things a `brief:` comment could not: it went through the `brief` op, which no MCP tool sends and a sign-in of scope `mcp` or `ci` is refused (`scopes.go`); who recorded it, from the key; and that the recorder was not the session that claimed the issue. What it cannot tell, within one principal, is the coordinator's process from an agent that runs `aries` by hand under the same key with a session of its choosing. On this project every session runs as one principal, so that remains the coordinator's judgment, as merges.md §6 says today. Two ways close it: workers run as a principal of their own role (decision 97), so the recorder's principal differs from the claimant's and the key proves it; or the guard denies `aries dispatch brief --record` to worker sessions, which narrows it but rests on the guard. Either way, M4 reads `show`'s brief, not a comment's first line.

*Decided 23 Oct 2026 (the maintainer, answering this PR's question):* "Decide (a), with (b) as defense in depth: M4 trusts a stored brief only once workers run as their own role principal; the guard also denies brief --record to workers." So the opener stays unverified until workers run as a principal of their own role (decision 97, aries-6tu2ypf2), and M4 trusts a stored brief only when its recorder's principal is not the claimant's. The guard's denial of `aries dispatch brief --record` to worker sessions is defense in depth, never the basis of trust; its rule is filed apart from this change, for the maintainer.

### 22.5 As built (23 Oct 2026)

- **Protocol 11.** `OpBrief` with `BriefArgs` (`id`, `harness`, `model`, `effort`, `session`, `template`, `text`, `idem`) and `BriefResult` (`id`, `sha256`, `claim`); `ShowResult.Brief`. Scopes: cli only.
- **Store.** `Store.RecordBrief` (`store/briefs.go`), the join in `start`'s write, `IssueView.Brief`, `Limits.BriefBytes`, and migration `0004_briefs.sql`.
- **CLI.** `aries dispatch brief ID --record --model M --session S [--effort E]` records the brief and prints only the text to send; with `--text FILE` (or `-`) it records the text given instead of rendering one, and `--template` names its version. `aries show` prints the brief's line. The dispatcher records each run's brief this way from its own session, naming the run's.
- **Not built.** M4's reading of it; a brief per run in `aries outcome`, which still reads the comment's line and so reads stored briefs as it read posted ones; export of the `briefs` table by `ariesd export-bd`, which exports the comment.
